\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\phi}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\lambda\mu\nu\rho}u_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}^{\Phi}_{\lambda,\mu\nu},

(117d)

and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S12}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left[\Delta^{\lambda}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}+\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\nu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\right]\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\Phi}^{\rho,\sigma\alpha},

(117e)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S12}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left[\Delta^{\lambda}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}+\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\nu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\right]\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\Phi}^{\rho,\sigma\alpha},

(117f)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S13}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left[\Delta^{\lambda}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}+\Delta^{\nu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\right]\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\Phi}^{\rho,\sigma\alpha},

(117g)

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S13}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\left[\Delta^{\lambda}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}+\Delta^{\nu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\nu}_{\alpha}-\Delta^{\mu}_{\rho}\Delta^{\nu}_{\sigma}\Delta^{\lambda}_{\alpha}\right]\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\Phi}^{\rho,\sigma\alpha}.

(117h)

The linear response theory of these operator result in

\Delta_{\Phi,\,{\rm LTE}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=f_{\rho}\,(2b_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{f}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}+\Upsilon_{\rho}(-2b_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}+I\,(2b_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I}\right)_{\rm LTE}\\
+(I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho})(-2b_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}+I_{S\,\rho\sigma}(2b_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I_{S}}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm LTE}\\
+\phi\,(b_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\phi}\right)_{\rm LTE}+\Phi^{\rm S12}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}(3b_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S12}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm LTE}+\Phi^{\rm S13}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}(3b_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S13}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm LTE}\\
\equiv f_{\rho}\,G^{\mu\rho}_{f,\,{\rm LTE}}++\Upsilon_{\rho}\,G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon,\,{\rm LTE}}+I\,G^{\mu}_{I,\,{\rm LTE}}+(I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho})\,G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon,\,{\rm LTE}}+I_{S\,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S},\,{\rm LTE}}\\
+\phi\,G^{\mu}_{\phi,\,{\rm LTE}}+\Phi^{\rm S12}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12,\,LTE}+\Phi^{\rm S13}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13,\,LTE},

(118)

for the local equilibrium part and in

\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi,\,{\rm D}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=&f_{\rho}\,(2c_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{f}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D}+\Upsilon_{\rho}(-2c_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D}+I\,(2c_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I}\right)_{\rm D}\\
&+(I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho})(-2c_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D}+I_{S\,\rho\sigma}(2c_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I_{S}}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D}\\
&+\phi\,(c_{\Phi})\,\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\phi}\right)_{\rm D}+\Phi^{\rm S12}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}(3c_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S12}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D}+\Phi^{\rm S13}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}(3c_{\Phi})\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S13}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D}\\
\equiv&f_{\rho}\,G^{\mu\rho}_{f,\,{\rm D}}++\Upsilon_{\rho}\,G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon,\,{\rm D}}+I\,G^{\mu}_{I,\,{\rm D}}+(I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho})\,G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon,\,{\rm D}}+I_{S\,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S},\,{\rm D}}\\
&+\phi\,G^{\mu}_{\phi,\,{\rm D}}+\Phi^{\rm S12}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12,\,D}+\Phi^{\rm S13}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\,G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13,\,D}\end{split}

(119)

for the dissipative part. We also decompose the contributions of the different components to the linear response of
\mathcal{A}^{\mu}
as

\begin{split}\Delta_{\Phi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=&\Delta_{f}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{\Upsilon}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{I}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{I-\Upsilon}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{I_{S}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{\phi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{\rm S12}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}+\Delta_{\rm S13}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}.\end{split}

(120)

Using the results from the previous sections, all possible vectors that can be built proportional to these components of
\Phi
are immediately obtained:

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{fu}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+a_{f\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+a_{fk}Q^{\mu\rho}+a_{f\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]f_{\rho}\equiv G_{f}^{\mu\rho}\,f_{\rho},

(121a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\Upsilon}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{\Upsilon u}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+a_{\Upsilon\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+a_{\Upsilon k}Q^{\mu\rho}+a_{\Upsilon\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\Upsilon_{\rho}\equiv G_{\Upsilon}^{\mu\rho}\,\Upsilon_{\rho},

(121b)

\displaystyle\Delta_{I}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{Iu}u^{\mu}+a_{Ik}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]I\equiv G_{I}^{\mu}\,I,

(121c)

\displaystyle\Delta_{I-\Upsilon}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{I-\Upsilon u}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+a_{I-\Upsilon\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+a_{I-\Upsilon k}Q^{\mu\rho}+a_{I-\Upsilon\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right](I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho})

(121d)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle G_{I-\Upsilon}^{\mu\rho}\,(I_{\rho}-\Upsilon_{\rho}),

\displaystyle\Delta_{I_{S}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{I_{S}u}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\mu}+a_{I_{S}\Delta}\frac{\Delta^{\mu\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}+a_{I_{S}k}\frac{Q^{\mu\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}+a_{I_{S}\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}\frac{u_{\nu}k^{\perp}_{\alpha}}{(k\cdot u)^{2}}\right]I_{S\,\rho\sigma}

(121e)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle G_{I_{S}}^{\mu\rho\sigma}\,I_{S\,\rho\sigma},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\phi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{\phi u}u^{\mu}+a_{\phi k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\phi\equiv G_{\phi}^{\mu}\,\phi,

(121f)

where

\displaystyle a_{fu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}u_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{f},

\displaystyle a_{f\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{f},

\displaystyle a_{fk}=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{f},

(122a)

\displaystyle a_{f\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}G^{\mu\rho}_{f},

\displaystyle a_{\Upsilon u}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}u_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{\Upsilon\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon},

(122b)

\displaystyle a_{\Upsilon k}=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{\Upsilon\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}G^{\mu\rho}_{\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{Iu}=

\displaystyle u_{\mu}G^{\mu}_{I},

(122c)

\displaystyle a_{Ik}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)k^{\perp}_{\mu}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}G^{\mu}_{I},

\displaystyle a_{I-\Upsilon u}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}u_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{I-\Upsilon\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon},

(122d)

\displaystyle a_{I-\Upsilon k}=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{I-\Upsilon\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}G^{\mu\rho}_{I-\Upsilon},

\displaystyle a_{I_{S}u}=

\displaystyle\frac{u_{\mu}k^{\perp}_{\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S}},

(122e)

and

\displaystyle a_{I_{S}\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{3k_{\perp}^{2}}\Delta_{\mu\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S}},

\displaystyle a_{I_{S}k}=

\displaystyle\frac{3(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}Q_{\mu\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S}},

\displaystyle a_{\phi u}=

\displaystyle u_{\mu}G^{\mu}_{\phi},

(122f)

\displaystyle a_{\phi k}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)k^{\perp}_{\mu}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}G^{\mu}_{\phi},

\displaystyle a_{I_{S}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{2(k_{\perp}^{2})^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}k_{\perp}^{\tau}G^{\mu\rho\sigma}_{I_{S}},

(122g)

and imposing
\Delta_{\Phi}\mathcal{A}\cdot k=0
, we must have

\displaystyle a_{fu}=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}a_{fk}-a_{f\Delta},

\displaystyle a_{\Upsilon u}=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}a_{\Upsilon k}-a_{\Upsilon\Delta},

(123)

\displaystyle a_{Ik}=

\displaystyle-a_{Iu}\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}},

\displaystyle a_{I-\Upsilon u}=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}a_{I-\Upsilon k}-a_{I-\Upsilon\Delta},

(124)

\displaystyle a_{I_{S}u}=

\displaystyle\left(\frac{2}{3}a_{I_{S}k}-a_{I_{S}\Delta}\right)\frac{k^{2}_{\perp}}{(k\cdot u)^{2}},

\displaystyle a_{\phi k}=

\displaystyle-a_{\phi u}\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}.

(125)

The parts that do not reduce to previous cases are the linear response to
\Phi_{S12}
and
\Phi_{S13}
.
In these cases the vectors that one can build are the followings:

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm S12}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{\rm{S12}\Delta}\Delta^{\tau\rho}\Delta^{\mu\sigma}+a_{\rm{S12}k}Q^{\tau\rho}\Delta^{\mu\sigma}+a_{\rm{S12}\Delta\epsilon}\Delta^{\mu\tau}\epsilon^{\lambda\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{u_{\lambda}k^{\perp}_{\nu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right.

(126a)

\displaystyle\left.+a_{\rm{S12}\epsilon}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\tau}u_{\nu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\Phi^{S12}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\equiv G_{\rm S12}^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}\,\Phi^{S12}_{\tau\rho\sigma},

(126b)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm S13}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[a_{\rm{S13}\Delta}\Delta^{\tau\sigma}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+a_{\rm{S13}k}Q^{\tau\sigma}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+a_{\rm{S13}\Delta\epsilon}\Delta^{\mu\tau}\epsilon^{\lambda\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{u_{\lambda}k^{\perp}_{\nu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right.

(126c)

\displaystyle\left.+a_{\rm{S13}\epsilon}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\tau}u_{\nu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\Phi^{S13}_{\tau,\rho\sigma}\equiv G_{\rm S13}^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}\,\Phi^{S13}_{\tau\rho\sigma},

(126d)

where

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S12}\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{9}\Delta_{\mu\sigma}\Delta_{\tau\rho}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12},

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S12}k}=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}Q_{\mu\sigma}Q_{\tau\rho}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12},

(127a)

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S12}\Delta\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\epsilon_{\tau\sigma\alpha\beta}k_{\perp}^{\alpha}u^{\beta}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12},

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S12}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)k^{\perp}_{\tau}k_{\perp}^{\alpha}u^{\beta}k^{\perp}_{\rho}}{2(k_{\perp})^{4}}\epsilon_{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S12},

(127b)

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S13}\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{9}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\Delta_{\tau\sigma}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13},

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S13}k}=

\displaystyle\frac{9}{4}Q_{\mu\rho}Q_{\tau\sigma}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13},

(127c)

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S13}\Delta\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\epsilon_{\tau\sigma\alpha\beta}k_{\perp}^{\alpha}u^{\beta}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13},

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S13}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)k^{\perp}_{\tau}k_{\perp}^{\alpha}u^{\beta}k^{\perp}_{\rho}}{2(k_{\perp})^{4}}\epsilon_{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}G^{\mu\tau\rho\sigma}_{\rm S13},

(127d)

and when
\Delta_{\Phi}\mathcal{A}\cdot k=0
, we have

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S12}\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}a_{\rm{S12}k},\quad a_{\rm{S12}\Delta\epsilon}=0,

(128a)

\displaystyle a_{\rm{S13}\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}a_{\rm{S13}k},\quad a_{\rm{S13}\Delta\epsilon}=0.

(128b)

4
Vector and axial Wigner functions in a dissipative fluid

This section derives the first-order gradient expansion of the axial and vector parts of the Wigner function resulting from the statistical operator in eq. (
11
). For the spin polarization, only
W_{+}
, i.e., the particle part of the Wigner function, is needed; however, because the decomposition has the same form for both the complete and the particle parts, the subscript
+
has been omitted. From the statistical operator (
11
), it follows that we have to consider the linear response theory of the following hydrodynamic fields:

\partial_{\rho}\zeta,\,\zeta_{A},\,\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{A},\,\varpi_{\rho\sigma},\,\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma},\,\xi_{\rho\sigma},\,\partial_{\tau}\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma}.

In order to apply the results obtained in the previous section, these hydrodynamic fields are further decomposed into their components of irreducible representation under rotations that keep
u
invariant. The gradient of the vector chemical potential is decomposed as

\partial^{\rho}\zeta=u^{\rho}D\zeta+r^{\rho},\quad r^{\rho}=\partial^{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta,

(129)

where
D=u^{\mu}\partial_{\mu}
,
V^{\langle\rho\rangle}=V^{\rho}_{\perp}=\Delta^{\rho}_{\lambda}V^{\lambda}
, and
\Delta^{\mu\nu}=\eta^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu}
(see the notation after the Introduction); similarly for the gradient of the axial chemical potential

\partial^{\rho}\zeta_{A}=u^{\rho}D\zeta_{A}+r_{A}^{\rho},\quad r_{A}^{\rho}=\partial^{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta_{A},

(130)

while
\zeta_{A}
is a scalar and does not need to be decomposed further.
The thermal vorticity, being an antisymmetric tensor, can be decomposed with two vectors:

\varpi_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\mu}\beta_{\nu}-\partial_{\nu}\beta_{\mu}\right)=\alpha_{\mu}u_{\nu}-\alpha_{\nu}u_{\mu}+\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}w^{\rho}u^{\sigma},

(131)

where the thermal acceleration
\alpha=|\beta|a
and the thermal angular velocity
w=|\beta|\omega
are obtained by

\alpha^{\mu}=\varpi^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\beta a^{\mu}-\partial_{\perp}^{\mu}\beta\right),\quad w^{\mu}={\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\varpi_{\rho\sigma}u_{\nu},

(132)

with
\beta=|\beta|=\sqrt{\beta^{2}}
, and
a^{\mu}=u\cdot\partial u^{\mu}
is the acceleration field, and the vectors are orthogonal to
u
:
u\cdot\alpha=u\cdot w=0
.
The spin potential is also an antisymmetric tensor, therefore it can be split into two three-vectors:

\mathfrak{S}_{\mu\nu}=\mathfrak{a}_{\mu}u_{\nu}-\mathfrak{a}_{\nu}u_{\mu}+\epsilon_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{w}^{\rho}u^{\sigma},

(133)

where the spin acceleration
\mathfrak{a}
and spin angular velocity
\mathfrak{w}
are defined by

\mathfrak{a}^{\mu}=\mathfrak{S}^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu},\quad\mathfrak{w}^{\mu}={\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma}u_{\nu}.

(134)

The gradient of the spin potential
\partial^{\lambda}\mathfrak{S}^{\mu\nu}
is a rank-3 spin like tensor. The decomposition of this type of tensor was derived in section
3.5
and results in:

\begin{split}\partial^{\lambda}\mathfrak{S}^{\mu\nu}=&u^{\lambda}\left(f^{\mu}u^{\nu}-f^{\nu}u^{\mu}\right)+\epsilon^{\lambda\mu\nu\rho}\Upsilon_{\rho}+\left(\Delta^{\lambda\mu}u^{\nu}-\Delta^{\lambda\nu}u^{\mu}\right)I\\
&+\left(\epsilon^{\lambda\mu\alpha\beta}u^{\nu}-\epsilon^{\lambda\nu\alpha\beta}u^{\mu}\right)u_{\alpha}(I_{\beta}-\Upsilon_{\beta})+\left(I_{S}^{\lambda\mu}u^{\nu}-I_{S}^{\lambda\nu}u^{\mu}\right)\\
&+\phi\,\epsilon^{\lambda\mu\nu\rho}u_{\rho}+\Phi_{S12}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}+\Phi_{S13}^{\lambda,\mu\nu}.\end{split}

(135)

Finally, the symmetric thermal shear

\xi_{\rho\sigma}=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\rho}\beta_{\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}\beta_{\rho}\right)

(136)

is decomposed as follows

\xi_{\rho\sigma}=u_{\rho}u_{\sigma}D\beta+\frac{\Delta_{\rho\sigma}}{3}\beta\theta+\frac{1}{2}\left(u_{\rho}\Delta^{\tau}_{\,\sigma}+u_{\sigma}\Delta^{\tau}_{\,\rho}\right)\left(\beta Du_{\tau}+\partial_{\tau}\beta\right)+\Delta^{\lambda\tau}_{\rho\sigma}\beta\sigma_{\lambda\tau},

(137)

where we remind that
\theta=\partial_{\mu}u^{\mu}
, and
\sigma
is the proper shear tensor, which is symmetric, traceless and orthogonal to
u
, that is

\sigma_{\rho\sigma}=\Delta_{\rho\sigma\mu\nu}\partial^{\mu}u^{\nu}=\left[\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{\mu\rho}\Delta_{\nu\sigma}+\Delta_{\mu\sigma}\Delta_{\nu\rho}\right)-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\nu}\Delta_{\rho\sigma}\right]\partial^{\mu}u^{\nu}.

(138)

Each of these components is considered a hydrodynamic field, denoted by
\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}
. The linear response
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}
and
\Delta_{\mathcal{U}}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
, corresponding to their local equilibrium and dissipative operators

\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{U}}=b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}(x)\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{(\alpha)},\quad\widehat{C}_{\mathcal{U}}=c_{\mathcal{U}}\int_{\Omega}{\rm d}\Omega\,\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}(x_{2})\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{(\alpha)}(x_{2}),

(139)

appearing in the statistical operator, are obtained in the following sections.

4.1
Vector and axial chemical potentials

The vector chemical potential
\zeta
is included at the leading order and it is contained in the statistical operator (
24
) that we use to compute the thermal correlators (
35
) and (
43
).
Consider now the axial chemical potential
\zeta_{A}
that is a scalar quantity. The corresponding operators, see eqs. (
18a
) and (
33b
), in the statistical operator are

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=X=\zeta_{A},\quad b_{\zeta_{A}}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\zeta_{A}}=\widehat{Q}_{A}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}(y),\\
c_{\zeta_{A}}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\zeta_{A}}=\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}_{A}.\end{split}

(140)

The gradient of the chemical potential is decomposed in eq. (
129
) and consist in a scalar part and a vector part. The linear response is obtained from eqs. (
18c
) and (
33a
); the scalar part is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=X=D\zeta,\quad b_{D\zeta}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{D\zeta}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\rho}u_{\rho}(x)\widehat{j}^{\lambda}(y),\\
c_{D\zeta}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D\zeta}(x_{2})=u_{\lambda}(x)\widehat{j}^{\lambda}(x_{2}),\end{split}

(141)

while the vector is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=V_{\rho}=r_{\rho},\quad b_{r}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r}^{\rho}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}(y),\\
c_{r}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{r}(x_{2})^{\rho}=\widehat{j}^{\langle\rho\rangle}(x_{2}).\end{split}

(142)

Similarly, for the gradient of the axial chemical potential we have the decomposition (
130
), and from eqs. (
18c
) and (
33b
) we have for the scalar part

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=X=D\zeta_{A},\quad b_{D\zeta_{A}}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{D\zeta_{A}}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\rho}u_{\rho}(x)\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}(y),\\
c_{D\zeta_{A}}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D\zeta_{A}}(x_{2})=u_{\rho}(x)\left[\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}(x_{2})+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}(x_{2})\right],\end{split}

(143)

and for the vector part

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=V_{\rho}=r_{A\,\rho},\quad b_{r_{A}}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r_{A}}^{\rho}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}(y),\\
c_{r_{A}}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{A}}(x_{2})^{\rho}=\widehat{j}_{A}^{\langle\rho\rangle}(x_{2})+(x_{2}-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}(x_{2}).\end{split}

(144)

Therefore, using the results of previous sections, the linear response theory of the axial and vector parts of the Wigner function can be written as

\begin{split}\Delta_{\zeta_{A},\partial\zeta,\partial\zeta_{A}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=&\left[a_{\zeta_{A}u}u^{\mu}+a_{\zeta_{A}k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\zeta_{A}+\left[\bar{a}_{D\zeta_{A}u}u^{\mu}+\bar{a}_{D\zeta_{A}k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\zeta_{A}\\
&+\left[\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{D\zeta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{D\zeta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\zeta\\
&+\left[\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{ru}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{r\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{rk}Q^{\mu\rho}+a^{c}_{r\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\partial_{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta\\
&+\left[\bar{a}_{r_{A}u}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{a}_{r_{A}\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{a}_{r_{A}k}Q^{\mu\rho}+\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\partial_{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta_{A},\end{split}

(145)

and

\begin{split}\Delta_{\zeta_{A},\partial\zeta,\partial\zeta_{A}}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}=&\left[\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\zeta_{A}u}u^{\mu}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\zeta_{A}k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\zeta_{A}+\left[\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{D\zeta_{A}u}u^{\mu}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{D\zeta_{A}k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\zeta_{A}\\
&+\left[\bar{v}_{D\zeta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{v}_{D\zeta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\zeta\\
&+\left[\bar{v}_{ru}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{v}_{r\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{v}_{rk}Q^{\mu\rho}+\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\partial_{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta\\
&+\left[\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{r_{A}u}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{r_{A}\Delta}^{c}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{r_{A}k}^{c}Q^{\mu\rho}+v_{r_{A}\epsilon}^{c}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\partial_{\langle\rho\rangle}\zeta_{A}.\end{split}

(146)

As explained in the notation section, the symbols used for the coefficients reflect their properties under discrete transformations
\mathsf{P}
,
\mathsf{T}
, and
\mathsf{C}
, according to table (
7
), which has been derived from eq. (
76
) and

\begin{array}[]{|l|ccc|cc|cc|cc|cc|cc|cc|}\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
&\zeta_{A}&D\zeta_{A}&\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{A}&D\zeta&\partial_{\rho}\zeta&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\zeta_{A}}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\zeta_{A}}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{D\zeta}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D\zeta}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{D\zeta_{A}}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D\zeta_{A}}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{r}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r_{A}}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{r_{A}}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&-&-&(-,+)&+&(+,-)&-&-&+&+&-&-&-&-&+&+\\
\mathsf{T}&+&-&(-,+)&-&(-,+)&+&-&-&+&-&+&+&-&+&-\\
\mathsf{C}&+&+&(+,+)&-&(-,-)&+&+&-&-&+&+&-&-&+&+\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(147)

The corresponding Kubo formulas, as derived in previous sections, are given by

\displaystyle a_{\zeta_{A}u}=

\displaystyle\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{Q}_{A}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\zeta_{A}u}=

\displaystyle\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{Q}_{A}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(148a)

\displaystyle a_{\zeta_{A}k}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{Q}_{A}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\zeta_{A}k}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{Q}_{A}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(148b)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{D\zeta u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{D\zeta u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(148c)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{D\zeta k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{D\zeta k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(148d)

and

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{D\zeta_{A}u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u_{\rho}(\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda})\right)_{\rm D},

(148e)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{D\zeta_{A}u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u_{\rho}(\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda})\right)_{\rm D},

(148f)

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{D\zeta_{A}k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u_{\rho}(\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda})\right)_{\rm D},

(148g)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{D\zeta_{A}k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u_{\rho}(\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda})\right)_{\rm D},

(148h)

and

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{ru}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{ru}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(149a)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{r\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{r\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(149b)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}^{c}_{rk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{rk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(149c)

\displaystyle a_{r\epsilon}^{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(149d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r_{A}}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle v^{c}_{r_{A}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{r_{A}}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(149e)

and

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{r_{A}u}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(149f)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{r_{A}u}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(149g)

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{r_{A}\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(149h)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}_{r_{A}\Delta}^{c}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(149i)

\displaystyle\bar{a}_{r_{A}k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D},

(149j)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{v}}^{c}_{r_{A}k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\rho}+(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}_{A}^{\lambda}\right)_{\rm D}.

(149k)

It is important to stress that the coefficients that are odd under time reversal, that we remind it means that they connect
\mathcal{A}^{\mu}
or
\mathcal{V}^{\mu}
with a vector with opposite parity under time reversal
T
, can not be obtained from the local thermal equilibrium operators, as it can be explicitly checked from the transformations listed in (
76
) and (
147
). Indeed, the equilibrium statistical operator used to evaluate the thermal correlators (
35
) and (
43
) has time reversal symmetry, from which follows that the coefficients can be non-vanishing only if the correlators is evaluated between quantities with the same parity under time reversal. It is always the case that only the operators from the dissipative part can give rise to odd time reversal coefficients and that is the reason we refer to them as dissipative. We also notice that all the dissipative corrections require a chiral coefficient or are sourced by the axial chemical potential.

The coefficient
a_{r\epsilon}^{c}
give the spin-Hall effect (SHE)
[
23
]
in spin polarization (also called the spin Nerst effect). As noted earlier, despite this coefficient is charged, it can be non-vanishing even at zero chemical potential
\zeta=0
. That is because it gives the particle part of the Wigner function and differs from charged coefficients giving the axial current
j^{\mu}_{A}
, which is instead obtained by integrating the particle and anti-particle
\mathcal{A}^{\mu}
over
k
. Moreover, we can identify the terms proportional to
\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}
,
\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}
and
v^{c}_{r_{A}\epsilon}
as analogous to the SHE and that we consequently call chiral spin-Hall (Nerst) effect, chiral electrical effect (CEE) and axial Hall effect (AHE), respectively. All of these effects are non-dissipative and half of them are chiral.

4.2
Thermal vorticity

We consider now the local equilibrium contributions from the thermal vorticity. The dissipative part will be included together with the spin potential. From eq. (
18a
), the operators associated with the vectors
w
and
\alpha
in the decomposition (
131
) are:

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=\Gamma_{\rho\sigma}=\varpi_{\rho\sigma},\quad b_{\varpi}=&\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\varpi}^{\rho\sigma}=\widehat{J}^{\rho\sigma}_{x},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\alpha}^{\rho}=\widehat{K}_{x}^{\rho},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{w}^{\rho}=\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho},\end{split}

(150)

where

\widehat{K}_{x}^{\rho}=u_{\lambda}\widehat{J}_{x}^{\lambda\rho},\quad\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\rho}u_{\alpha}\widehat{J}_{x\,\beta\gamma}

(151)

are the boost and the angular momentum operators.
To make the notation more compact we define the following axial vectors

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{w\Delta}=

\displaystyle w^{\mu}=\Delta^{\mu\rho}w_{\rho},

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot w)}{(k\cdot u)}u^{\mu}=\frac{k^{\rho}_{\perp}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}w_{\rho},

(152a)

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\alpha_{\rho},

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{wk}=

\displaystyle Q^{\mu\rho}w_{\rho},

(152b)

and the following acceleration vectors:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle\alpha^{\mu}=\Delta^{\mu\rho}\alpha_{\rho},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha u}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot\alpha)}{(k\cdot u)}u^{\mu}=\frac{k^{\rho}_{\perp}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\alpha_{\rho},

(153a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{w\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}w_{\rho},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha k}=

\displaystyle Q^{\mu\rho}\alpha_{\rho}\,.

(153b)

According to the transformation properties:

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|cc|}\hline\cr&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
&w^{\mu},\,A_{X}^{\mu}&a^{\mu},\,\mathfrak{A}_{X}^{\mu}&\widehat{K}^{\langle\rho\rangle}&\widehat{J}^{\langle\rho\rangle}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&(-,+)&(+,-)&-&+\\
\mathsf{T}&(+,-)&(-,+)&+&-\\
\mathsf{C}&(+,+)&(+,+)&+&+\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(154)

the linear response to thermal vorticity for the axial and vector part of Wigner function results in

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle a_{w\Delta}A^{\mu}_{w\Delta}-a_{wu}A^{\mu}_{wu}+a_{\alpha\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}+a_{wk}A^{\mu}_{wk}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha\Delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha\Delta}

(155)

\displaystyle-\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha u}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{w\epsilon}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{w\epsilon}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha k},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{w\Delta}A^{\mu}_{w\Delta}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wu}A^{\mu}_{wu}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\alpha\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wk}A^{\mu}_{wk}

(156)

\displaystyle+\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha\Delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha\Delta}-\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha u}+\bar{v}^{c}_{w\epsilon}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{w\epsilon}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\alpha k}.

From previous results, their Kubo formulas are:

\displaystyle a_{wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha u}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157a)

\displaystyle a_{w\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157b)

\displaystyle a_{wk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\alpha k}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157c)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{w\epsilon}=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle a_{\alpha\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{K}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(157d)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha u}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157e)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{w\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157f)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\alpha k}=

\displaystyle 0,

(157g)

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{w\epsilon}=

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\alpha\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{K}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(157h)

The contributions from thermal vorticity are usually written in a different form. Taking advantage of the identity

{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\varpi_{\alpha\beta}k_{\nu}=-(k\cdot u)\left[w^{\mu}-\frac{(k\cdot w)}{(k\cdot u)}u^{\mu}+\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\alpha_{\rho}\right],

(158)

we can re-write the Wigner functions as

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle-a_{\varpi}\frac{2{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(a_{wu}-a_{w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{wu}+(a_{\alpha\epsilon}-a_{w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}+a_{wk}A^{\mu}_{wk},

(159)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\varpi}\frac{2{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wu}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{wu}+(\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\alpha\epsilon}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{wk}A^{\mu}_{wk},

(160)

where

\displaystyle a_{\varpi}=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2(k\cdot u)}a_{w\Delta}=-\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}=-\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x\,\mu}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(161a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\varpi}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{J}_{x\,\mu}\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(161b)

The coefficients
a_{\varpi}
and
\mathfrak{v}^{c}
are the one giving the axial vortical effect and the chiral vortical effect. The numerical factors defining
a_{\varpi}
have been chosen such that for a free Dirac field the coefficient assumes the simple form:

a_{\varpi}^{\rm Free}=\delta(k^{2}-m^{2})\theta(k\cdot u)n_{F}(\beta\cdot k)\left(1-n_{F}(\beta\cdot k)\right),

(162)

with
n_{F}
the Fermi-Dirac distribution function
n_{F}(z)=({\rm e}^{z}+1)^{-1}
.
For a massive field, the spin polarization is directly related to the axial part of the Wigner function.
In that case, it must satisfy
k\cdot\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}(x,k)=0
, so:

\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=-a_{\varpi}\frac{2{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu}k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(a_{wu}-a_{w\Delta})\left(A^{\mu}_{wu}+\frac{3}{2}A^{\mu}_{wk}\right)+(a_{\alpha\epsilon}-a_{w\delta})A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}\,.

(163)

As a check it will be shown that the equations above can reproduce the known conductivity of the axial vortical effect. Since the coefficients are Lorentz scalars, without loss of generality they can be evaluated in the local rest frame where
u=(1,\,\bm{0})
. In this frame, the coefficient
a_{\varpi}
is given by

a_{\varpi}=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\sum_{i}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{i}(0,k),\,\widehat{J}^{i}\right)_{{\rm LTE},T},

(164)

where we used the translation invariance of the correlator. By invariance we just need to compute one component:

a_{\varpi}=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2(k\cdot u)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{z}(0,k),\,\widehat{J}^{z}\right)_{{\rm LTE},T}=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2(k\cdot u)}\int_{0}^{|\beta(x)|}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta(x)|}\langle\widehat{J}^{z}_{[\tau/\beta]}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{z}(0,k)\rangle_{T,c}\,,

(165)

where we used the definition (
43
). By integrating in
k
we obtain the axial current, which result in the axial vortical effect, that is an axial current induced by rotation:

\Delta_{\varpi}j_{A}^{\mu}(x)=\int{\rm d}^{4}k\,\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=\frac{1}{2}\varpi_{\rho\sigma}(x)\left(\widehat{J}^{\rho\sigma}(y),\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\mu}(0)\right)_{\beta(x)}=W_{A}(x)w^{\mu}(x)+\dots

(166)

From the equation above, the AVE conductivity
W_{A}
is obtained with

W_{A}=\frac{w_{\mu}}{w^{2}}\int{\rm d}^{4}k\,\Delta_{w}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k).

(167)

From eq. (
159
), since
A^{\mu}_{\alpha\epsilon}
is the response to acceleration and
w_{\mu}A^{\mu}_{wu}=w_{\mu}A^{\mu}_{wk}=0
, we are only left with

\begin{split}W_{A}=&-\frac{w_{\mu}\varpi_{\rho\sigma}}{w^{2}}\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,k_{\nu}\,a_{\varpi}(x,k)\\
=&2\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,(k\cdot u)\,a_{\varpi}(x,k)=\int{\rm d}^{4}k\int_{0}^{\beta}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta|}\langle\widehat{J}^{z}_{[\tau/\beta]}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{z}(0,k)\rangle_{T,c}\,.\end{split}

(168)

For the free field, considering first the particle part, we obtain

\begin{split}W^{+}_{A}=&2\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,(k\cdot u)\,a^{\rm Free}_{\varpi}(x,k)\\
=&\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,2(k\cdot u)\,\delta(k^{2}-m^{2})\theta(k\cdot u)n_{F}(\beta\cdot k)\left(1-n_{F}(\beta\cdot k)\right)\\
=&\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,n_{F}(|\beta|E_{k})\left(1-n_{F}(|\beta|E_{k})\right)=-\frac{1}{|\beta|}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,\frac{\partial}{\partial E_{k}}n_{F}(|\beta|E_{k})\\
=&\frac{1}{2\pi|\beta|}\int{\rm d}k\,\frac{2E_{k}^{2}-m^{2}}{E_{k}}n_{F}(|\beta|E_{k}).\end{split}

(169)

Then, adding the anti-particle part and including the chemical potential, we obtain the well known result
[
73
]

W_{A}=\frac{1}{2\pi|\beta|}\int{\rm d}k\,\frac{2E_{k}^{2}-m^{2}}{E_{k}}\left[n_{F}(|\beta|(E_{k}-\mu))+n_{F}(|\beta|(E_{k}+\mu))\right].

(170)

4.3
Spin potential

The local thermal equilibrium and the dissipative contributions of the spin potential are actually proportional to the difference between the spin potential and the thermal vorticity. The operators (
18b
), (
33c
) and (
33d
) in terms of the vectors
w
,
\mathfrak{w}
,
\alpha
and
\mathfrak{a}
from the decompositions (
131
) and (
133
), for
\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=\Gamma_{\rho\sigma}=\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma}-\varpi_{\rho\sigma}
are written as:

\begin{split}b_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}=&\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}^{\rho\sigma}=\widehat{S}^{\rho\sigma}_{x},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}=u_{\lambda}\widehat{S}^{\lambda\rho},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\rho}u_{\alpha}\widehat{S}_{\beta\gamma},\\
c_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}(x_{2})=\widehat{T}_{A}^{\rho\sigma}(x_{2}),\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}=u_{\lambda}\widehat{T}_{A}^{\lambda\rho},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}=\frac{1}{2}\epsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma\rho}u_{\alpha}\widehat{T}_{A\,\beta\gamma},\end{split}

(171)

where
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}
are the spin part of the total boost
\widehat{K}
and angular moment
\widehat{J}
operators. As done for thermal vorticity, we define the following axial vectors

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mu\rho}(\mathfrak{w}-w)_{\rho},

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{\rho}_{\perp}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}(\mathfrak{w}-w)_{\rho},

(172a)

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}(\mathfrak{a}-\alpha)_{\rho},

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}=

\displaystyle Q^{\mu\rho}(\mathfrak{w}-w)_{\rho},

(172b)

and the following acceleration vectors:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mu\rho}(\mathfrak{a}-\alpha)_{\rho},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{\rho}_{\perp}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}(\mathfrak{a}-\alpha)_{\rho},

(173a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}(\mathfrak{w}-w)_{\rho},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}=

\displaystyle Q^{\mu\rho}(\mathfrak{a}-\alpha)_{\rho}.

(173b)

According to the transformation properties:

\begin{array}[]{|c|c|c|cc|cc|}\hline\cr&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
&w^{\mu},\,A_{X}^{\mu}&a^{\mu},\,\mathfrak{A}_{X}^{\mu}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\langle\rho\rangle}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\langle\rho\rangle}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\langle\rho\rangle}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\langle\rho\rangle}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&(-,+)&(+,-)&-&+&-&+\\
\mathsf{T}&(+,-)&(-,+)&+&-&-&+\\
\mathsf{C}&(+,+)&(+,+)&+&+&+&+\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(174)

the linear response to the operators (
171
) for the axial and vector parts of the Wigner function is

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}-a_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}+a_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}+a_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}

(175)

\displaystyle+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}-\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}

(176)

\displaystyle+\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}-\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}.

The Kubo formulas for the coefficients are:

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}=

\displaystyle 2\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177a)

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}x^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}x^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177b)

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}=

\displaystyle-3Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177c)

\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}=-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},\,a_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}=-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(177d)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha u}=

\displaystyle 2\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177e)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\Delta}=

\displaystyle-\frac{2}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177f)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha k}=

\displaystyle-3Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(177g)

\bar{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\epsilon}=-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},\,\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}=-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(177h)

Note that, in this case as well, all dissipative corrections to the axial Wigner function require chiral coefficients. As done before for the thermal vorticity, the Wigner functions can also be written as

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi,{\rm LTE}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle-a_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}\frac{2({\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{\mu\nu}-{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu})k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(a_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}-a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}

(178)

\displaystyle+(a_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}-a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}+a_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk},

\displaystyle\Delta_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi,{\rm LTE}}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}\frac{2({\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{\mu\nu}-{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu})k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}

(179)

\displaystyle+(\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}-\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta})A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk},

where

\displaystyle a_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}=

\displaystyle\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2(k\cdot u)}a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta}=-\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\mu}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(180a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{6(k\cdot u)}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{\mu},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathfrak{w}-w}^{\mu}\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(180b)

For a massive field, the spin polarization is directly related to the axial part of the Wigner function.
In that case, it must satisfy
k\cdot\Delta_{\varpi}\mathcal{A}(x,k)=0
and we can write:

\begin{split}\Delta_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi,{\rm LTE}}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}(x,k)=&-a_{\mathfrak{S}-\varpi}\frac{2({\widetilde{\mathfrak{S}}}^{\mu\nu}-{\widetilde{\varpi}}^{\mu\nu})k_{\nu}}{(2\pi)^{3}}-(a_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}-a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\Delta})\left(A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wu}+\frac{3}{2}A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{w}-wk}\right)\\
&+(a_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}-a_{\mathfrak{w}-w\delta})A^{\mu}_{\mathfrak{a}-\alpha\epsilon}\,.\end{split}

(181)

4.4
Thermal shear

Using the decomposition of the thermal shear tensor (
137
), the non-dissipative part (
18a
) can be written as

\widehat{B}_{\xi}=-\frac{1}{2}\xi_{\rho\sigma}(x)\widehat{\Xi}_{x}^{\rho\sigma}=-\frac{1}{2}\left[\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\Xi}D\beta-\widehat{p}_{\Xi}\beta\theta+\widehat{q}_{\Xi}^{\sigma}\left(\beta Du_{\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}\beta\right)+\widehat{\pi}_{\Xi}^{\alpha\tau}\beta\sigma_{\alpha\tau}\right],

(182)

where

\widehat{\Xi}_{x}^{\rho\sigma}(\Sigma)=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\left[(y-x)^{\rho}\widehat{T}^{\lambda\sigma}(y)+(y-x)^{\sigma}\widehat{T}^{\lambda\rho}(y)\right]

(183)

and we defined

\begin{split}\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\Xi}=&u_{\mu}u_{\nu}\widehat{\Xi}^{\mu\nu},\quad\hat{p}_{\Xi}=-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\nu}\widehat{\Xi}^{\mu\nu},\quad\widehat{\pi}_{\Xi}^{\mu\nu}=\Delta_{\alpha\beta}^{\mu\nu}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha\beta},\quad\widehat{q}_{\Xi}^{\mu}=u_{\alpha}\Delta_{\beta}^{\mu}\widehat{\Xi}^{\alpha\beta}.\end{split}

(184)

For the dissipative part (
33a
) we have instead:

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\xi}(x_{2})=\widehat{\varepsilon}D\beta-\widehat{p}\beta\theta+\widehat{q}^{\sigma}\left(\beta Du_{\sigma}+\partial_{\sigma}\beta\right)+\widehat{\pi}^{\alpha\tau}\beta\sigma_{\alpha\tau},

(185)

where

\begin{split}\widehat{\varepsilon}=&u_{\mu}u_{\nu}\widehat{T}^{\mu\nu},\quad\hat{p}=-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\nu}\widehat{T}^{\mu\nu},\widehat{\pi}^{\mu\nu}=\Delta_{\alpha\beta}^{\mu\nu}\widehat{T}^{\alpha\beta},\quad\widehat{q}^{\mu}=u_{\alpha}\Delta_{\beta}^{\mu}\widehat{T}_{S}^{\alpha\beta}.\end{split}

(186)

The contributions from thermal shear are then divided into two scalars, one vector and one spin-1 symmetric contributions. The scalar one proportional to gradients of temperature is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=X=D\beta,\quad b_{D\beta}=&-\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{D\beta}=\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\Xi},\\
c_{D\beta}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{D\beta}(x_{2})=\widehat{\varepsilon}(x_{2}),\end{split}

(187)

the one proportional to the expansion rate is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=X=\beta\theta,\quad b_{\theta}=&+\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\theta}=\hat{p}_{\Xi},\\
c_{\theta}=&-1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\theta}(x_{2})=\hat{p}(x_{2}),\end{split}

(188)

the vector one is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=V_{\rho}=\beta Du_{\rho}+\partial_{\rho}\beta,\quad b_{q}=&-\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{q}=\widehat{q}_{\Xi}^{\rho},\\
c_{q}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{q}(x_{2})=\widehat{q}^{\rho}(x_{2}),\end{split}

(189)

and finally the one proportional to the shear tensor is

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=\Sigma_{\rho\sigma}=\beta\sigma_{\rho\sigma},\quad b_{\sigma}=&-\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\sigma}^{\rho\sigma}=\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}_{\Xi},\\
c_{\sigma}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\sigma}(x_{2})=\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}(x_{2}).\end{split}

(190)

After introducing the vectors

\displaystyle A^{\mu}_{\sigma\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\alpha\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}\frac{u_{\nu}k_{\alpha}}{(k\cdot u)}\beta\sigma_{\rho\sigma},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma u}=

\displaystyle(k\cdot u)\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\mu}\,\beta\sigma_{\rho\sigma},

(191a)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma\Delta}=

\displaystyle\Delta^{\mu\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}\beta\sigma_{\rho\sigma},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma k}=

\displaystyle Q^{\mu\rho}k_{\perp}^{\sigma}\,\beta\sigma_{\rho\sigma},

(191b)

that transform as follows

\begin{array}[]{|l|ccc|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|c|}\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
&\beta&D\beta&\theta&Du^{\langle\mu\rangle},\,\partial^{\langle\mu\rangle}\beta&\mathcal{A}^{\mu},\,A^{\mu}_{\sigma\epsilon}&a^{\mu},\,\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma X}&\widehat{\varepsilon}_{\Xi},\,\widehat{p}_{\Xi}&\widehat{\varepsilon},\,\widehat{p}&\widehat{q}^{\langle\mu\rangle}_{\Xi}&\widehat{q}^{\langle\mu\rangle}&\widehat{\pi}_{\Xi}^{\rho\sigma}&\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&+&+&+&-&(-,+)&(+,-)&+&+&-&-&+&+\\
\mathsf{T}&+&-&-&+&(+,-)&(-,+)&-&+&+&-&-&+\\
\mathsf{C}&+&+&+&+&(+,+)&(+,+)&+&+&+&+&+&+\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(192)

the linear response of the thermal shear is

\displaystyle\Delta_{D\beta}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{D\beta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{D\beta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\beta,

\displaystyle\Delta_{D\beta}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{v}^{c}_{D\beta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{v}^{c}_{D\beta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]D\beta,

(193a)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\theta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\theta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\beta\theta,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{v}^{c}_{\theta u}u^{\mu}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\theta k}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}\right]\beta\theta,

(193b)

\Delta_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma u}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma\Delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma\Delta}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma k}+a_{\sigma\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\sigma\epsilon},\,\Delta_{\sigma}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}=\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma u}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma u}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma\delta}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma\Delta}+\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma k}\mathfrak{A}^{\mu}_{\sigma k}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\sigma\epsilon}A^{\mu}_{\sigma\epsilon},

(193c)

and

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{qu}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{q\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{qk}Q^{\mu\rho}+a_{q\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right](\beta Du_{\rho}+\partial_{\rho}\beta),

(193d)

\displaystyle\Delta_{q}\mathcal{V}^{\mu}=

\displaystyle\left[\bar{v}^{c}_{qu}\frac{k_{\perp}^{\rho}u^{\mu}}{(k\cdot u)}+\bar{v}^{c}_{q\Delta}\Delta^{\mu\rho}+\bar{v}^{c}_{qk}Q^{\mu\rho}+\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{q\epsilon}\,\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\right](\beta Du_{\rho}+\partial_{\rho}\beta).

(193e)

The coefficients are obtained with the Kubo formulas:

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{D\beta u}=

\displaystyle\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\varepsilon}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{D\beta k}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\varepsilon}\right)_{\rm D},

(194a)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\theta u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{p}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\theta k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{p}\right)_{\rm D},

(194b)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{qu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{q\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(194c)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{qk}=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle a_{q\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{4k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{q}_{\Xi}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(194d)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma u}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{3k_{\perp}^{2}}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

(194e)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{\sigma k}=

\displaystyle\frac{3(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle a_{\sigma\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{4(k_{\perp}^{2})^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\pi}_{\Xi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(194f)

and

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{D\beta u}=

\displaystyle\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\varepsilon}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{D\beta k}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{\varepsilon}\right)_{\rm D},

(195a)

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\theta u}=

\displaystyle-\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{p}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\theta k}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(k^{\perp}_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{p}\right)_{\rm D},

(195b)

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{a}}_{qu}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{q\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

(195c)

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{qk}=

\displaystyle\frac{3}{2}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,\widehat{q}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{q\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)}{4k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{q}_{\Xi}^{\rho}\right)_{\rm LTE},

(195d)

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma u}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\left(u_{\mu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma\Delta}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{3k_{\perp}^{2}}\Delta_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

(195e)

\displaystyle\bar{v}^{c}_{\sigma k}=

\displaystyle\frac{3(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}Q_{\mu\rho}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,k^{\perp}_{\sigma}\widehat{\pi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm D},

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}^{c}_{\sigma\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{4(k_{\perp}^{2})^{2}}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu},\,u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k^{\perp}_{\sigma}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\widehat{\pi}_{\Xi}^{\rho\sigma}\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(195f)

We again stress that all the dissipative corrections are given in terms of chiral coefficients.

4.5
Gradients of spin potential

The operators related to the linear response of the gradient of the spin potential are given by (
18b
) and (
33b
), i.e.,

\begin{split}\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}=\Phi_{\tau,\rho\sigma}=\partial_{\tau}\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma},\quad b_{\partial\mathfrak{S}}=&1,\quad\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\partial\mathfrak{S}}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}=\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\tau}\widehat{S}^{\lambda,\rho\sigma}(y),\\
c_{\partial\mathfrak{S}}=&-\frac{1}{2},\quad\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\partial\mathfrak{S}}(x_{2})=\widehat{S}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}(x_{2})-2(x_{2}-x)^{\tau}\widehat{T}^{\rho\sigma}_{A}(x_{2}).\end{split}

(196)

In eq. (
135
) we decomposed
\partial_{\tau}\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma}
in terms of
f,\,\Upsilon,\,I,\,I-\Upsilon,\,I_{S},\,\phi
,
\Phi_{\rm S12}
and
\Phi_{\rm S13}
. Correspondingly, the eq. (
196
) defines the operators

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{f}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I_{S}}^{\rho\sigma},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\phi},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S12}^{\tau,\rho\sigma},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\rm S13}^{\tau,\rho\sigma},

(197)

and

\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{f}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I_{S}}^{\rho\sigma},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\phi},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S12}^{\tau,\rho\sigma},\,\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\rm S13}^{\tau,\rho\sigma}.

(198)

These hydrodynamic fields and the corresponding operators transform as follows:

\begin{array}[]{|l|ccc|ccc|ccc|ccc|}\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]
&f^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{f}^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{f}^{\rho}&\Upsilon^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{\Upsilon}^{\rho}&I&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I}&I^{\rho}-\Upsilon^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho}&\widehat{\mathcal{C}}_{I-\Upsilon}^{\rho}\\
\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&-&-&-&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+\\
\mathsf{T}&-&-&+&+&+&-&+&+&-&+&+&-\\
\mathsf{C}&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+&+\\
\hline\cr\end{array}

(199)

\begin{array}[]{|l|ccc|ccc|ccc|ccc|}\hline\cr&&&&&&&&&&&&\\[-10.00002pt]

\hline\cr\mathsf{P}&+&+&+&-&-&-&-&-&-&-&-&-\\
\mathsf{T}&+&+&-&-&-&+&-&-&+&-&-&+\\

(200)

\displaystyle\Delta_{f}\mathcal{A}^{\mu}=

(201g)
