I am grateful to V. Ambrus, F. Becattini, M. Chernodub, D. Roselli and E. Grossi for many fruitful discussions.
This work was supported by the European Union - NextGenerationEU through grant No. 760079/23.05.2023, funded by the Romanian Ministry of Research, Innovation and Digitization through Romania’s National Recovery and Resilience Plan, call no. PNRR-III-C9-2022-I8.

Appendix A
Kubo formulas in momentum space

This appendix expresses the correlation functions (Kubo formulas) obtained with linear response theory in coordinate space as retarded Green’s functions in momentum space.
For the dissipative part we obtained the correlator in eq. (
35
) that is

\left(\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}\right)_{\rm D}=\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{t}{\rm d}^{4}x_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(s,\,\bm{x}_{2})\right]\right\rangle_{\beta(x)}.

(239)

Since all the transport coefficient obtained are scalar quantities under Lorentz transformations, we compute this correlator in the local rest frame where
u=(1,\bm{0})
.
It is convenient to introduce a positive frequency
\omega^{\prime}
inside the time integrals and obtain the correlator in the limit
\omega^{\prime}\to 0
. We then consider the functional

C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{t}{\rm d}^{4}x_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega^{\prime}(t-t_{2})}\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(s,\,\bm{x}_{2})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}.

(240)

Noticing that
s<t_{2}<t
, we can express the thermal correlator as the retarded Green’s function

G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(t-s,\,\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{2})=-{\rm i}\theta(t-s)\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(s,\bm{x}_{2})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)},

(241)

obtaining

C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{t}{\rm d}^{4}x_{2}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega^{\prime}(t-t_{2})}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(t-s,\,\bm{x}-\bm{x}_{2}).

(242)

With a shift of integration variables:
\bm{x}_{2}\to\bm{x}_{2}-\bm{x}
,
t_{2}\to t_{2}-t
and
s\to s-t_{2}
, we obtain

C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{\beta}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\omega^{\prime}t_{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\int{\rm d}^{3}\bm{x}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(-s,\,-\bm{x}_{2}),

(243)

which can be expressed as the Fourier transform of the retarded Green’s function

G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(t,\bm{x})=\int\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}(\omega t-\bm{p}\cdot\bm{x})}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p}).

(244)

In the large volume limit we have

\int{\rm d}^{3}\bm{x}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(-s,\,-\bm{x}_{2})=\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\int\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega s}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})

(245)

from which follows that

C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=-\frac{1}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\int\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\rm d}t_{2}\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\omega^{\prime}t_{2}}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega s}.

(246)

The integral in
s
can be computed with a shift
\omega\to\omega-{\rm i}\delta
, with a positive
\delta
and taking the limit
\delta\to 0^{+}
; we obtain

\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega s}=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\int_{-\infty}^{t_{2}}{\rm d}s\,{\rm e}^{({\rm i}\omega+\delta)s}=\lim_{\delta\to 0^{+}}\frac{{\rm e}^{({\rm i}\omega+\delta)t_{2}}}{{\rm i}\omega+\delta}=-\frac{{\rm i}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\omega t_{2}}}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}},

(247)

and thus

\begin{split}C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=&\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\int\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\rm d}t_{2}\,\frac{{\rm i}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}(\omega-\omega^{\prime})t_{2}}}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}}\\
=&\frac{1}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\int\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\frac{1}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}-{\rm i}0^{+}}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})\\
=&\frac{1}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\oint\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\frac{1}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}-{\rm i}0^{+}}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p}),\end{split}

(248)

where the path in the complex integral is closed in the upper half-plane where the retarded Green’s function is analytic. We also assume that the retarded Green’s function goes to zero faster than
\omega^{-1}
such that the upper half-circle vanishes at infinity. The complex integral can then be computed with the residue theorem and our integrand has only two poles. We obtain

\begin{split}C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega^{\prime})=&\frac{2\pi{\rm i}}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\left[\text{Res}\left(\frac{1}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})}{2\pi}\right)_{\omega=\omega^{\prime}+{\rm i}0^{+}}\right.\\
&+\left.\text{Res}\left(\frac{1}{\omega-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{1}{\omega-\omega^{\prime}-{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})}{2\pi}\right)_{\omega=+{\rm i}0^{+}}\right]\\
=&\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\frac{G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega^{\prime},\,\bm{p})-G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(0,\,\bm{p})}{\omega^{\prime}}.\end{split}

(249)

Now, in the limit of vanishing
\omega^{\prime}
, we finally obtain

\left(\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}\right)_{\rm D}=\lim_{\omega^{\prime}\to 0}\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\frac{G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega^{\prime},\,\bm{p})-G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(0,\,\bm{p})}{\omega^{\prime}}=\frac{{\rm i}}{\beta}\left.\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\omega}\right|_{\omega=0}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p}).

(250)

Since the operators
\widehat{X}
and
\widehat{Y}
are hermitian, the thermal correlator satisfies the property

\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(s,\,\bm{x}_{2})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}^{*}=-\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(s,\,\bm{x}_{2})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}

(251)

from which follows that the retarded Green’s function in eq. (
241
) is real, that

\left\{\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})\right\}^{*}=\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(-\omega,\,\bm{p}),\quad\left\{C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,\omega)\right\}^{*}=C[\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}](x;\,-\omega)

(252)

and that

\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\text{Re}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(-\omega,\,\bm{p})=\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\text{Re}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p}),\quad\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\text{Im}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(-\omega,\,\bm{p})=-\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\text{Im}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p}).

(253)

Since the real (imaginary) part of the retarded Green’s function is an even (odd) function of
\omega
we can write

\left(\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}\right)_{\rm D}=-\frac{1}{\beta}\left.\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\omega}\right|_{\omega=0}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\text{Im }G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})

(254)

or in a covariant form

\left(\widehat{X},\,\widehat{Y}\right)_{\rm D}=-\frac{1}{|\beta(x)|}u^{\lambda}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\lambda}}\right|_{u\cdot p=0}\lim_{p_{\perp}\to 0}{\rm Im}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}}(p).

(255)

For the dissipative part proportional to the gradients of the axial chemical potential and of the spin potential we also have the operators

-\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{A}(x)\int{\rm d}^{4}x\,(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\partial_{\lambda}\widehat{j}^{\lambda}_{A},\quad\partial_{\tau}\mathfrak{S}_{\rho\sigma}(x)\int{\rm d}^{4}x\,(x_{2}-x)^{\tau}\widehat{T}^{\rho\sigma}_{A}(x_{2})

(256)

that we write generically as

\mathcal{U}_{\rho(\alpha_{2})}(x)\int{\rm d}^{4}x\,(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}.

(257)

Even in these cases, we can use the previous formula with the Green function

G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}x^{\rho}}(x)=-{\rm i}\theta(t)\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(0)\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}x^{\rho};

(258)

however, it might be convenient to split the operators from the coordinate
x
and express the correlator through the Green function:

G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}}(x)=-{\rm i}\theta(t)\left\langle\left[\widehat{X}(x),\,\widehat{Y}(0)\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}.

(259)

It is straightforward to show that

G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}x^{\rho}}(p)=-{\rm i}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}}(p),

(260)

from which immediately follows that

\left(\widehat{X},\,\int{\rm d}^{4}x\,(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}\right)_{\rm D}=\frac{1}{\beta}\left.\frac{{\rm d}}{{\rm d}\omega}\right|_{\omega=0}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}}(\omega,\,\bm{p})

(261)

and in covariant form

\left(\widehat{X},\,\int{\rm d}^{4}x\,(x_{2}-x)^{\rho}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}\right)_{\rm D}=-\frac{1}{|\beta(x)|}u^{\lambda}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\lambda}}\right|_{u\cdot p=0}\lim_{p_{\perp}\to 0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}{\rm Re}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{X}\widehat{Y}^{(\alpha_{2})}}(p).

(262)

Consider now the linear response of a local equilibrium operator in the local rest frame

\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{U}}^{(1)}=b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\mathcal{U}_{\rho(\alpha_{2})}(x)\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\rho(\alpha_{2})}=b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\mathcal{U}_{\rho(\alpha_{2})}(x)\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\rho}\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(y)

(263)

that leads to

\Delta_{\mathcal{U},1,\,{\rm LTE}}O(x)=\mathcal{U}_{\rho(\alpha_{2})}(x)\,b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{\rho(\alpha_{2})}\right)_{\rm LTE}

(264)

with

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\int_{0}^{|\beta|}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta(x)|}\langle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1[\tau/|\beta|]}^{(\alpha)}\widehat{O}(x)\rangle_{T(x),\,{\rm c}}\\
=&\int_{0}^{|\beta|}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,(y-x)^{\rho}\langle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}[\tau/|\beta|]}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(y)\widehat{O}(x)\rangle_{T(x),\,{\rm c}}\,.\end{split}

(265)

Considering that the operator
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}
commutes with
\widehat{Q}
, such that

\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}[z]}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(y)={\rm e}^{z\left(\beta(x)\cdot\widehat{P}-\zeta(x)\widehat{Q}\right)}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(y){\rm e}^{-z\left(\beta(x)\cdot\widehat{P}-\zeta(x)\widehat{Q}\right)}=\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}\left(y-{\rm i}z\sqrt{\beta(x)^{2}}\right),

(266)

and that the correlation between the operator
\widehat{O}
at time
t
and
\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}
at the infinitely remote past is vanishing, we obtain

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\frac{{\rm i}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{0}{\rm d}t_{2}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)(y-x)^{\rho}\left\langle\left[\widehat{O}(x),\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(t_{2}+y_{0},\,\bm{y})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}.\end{split}

(267)

We now replace the thermal average of the commutator with its spectral function in Fourier space

\left\langle\left[\widehat{O}(x),\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}(y_{0}+t_{2},\,\bm{y})\right]\right\rangle_{T(x)}=\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p_{0}t_{2}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p)

(268)

obtaining

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\frac{{\rm i}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}t_{2}\,\theta(-t_{2})\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p_{0}t_{2}}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)(y-x)^{\rho}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}.\end{split}

(269)

We replace the Heaviside theta with its integral representation and we integrate in
t_{2}
:

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}t_{2}\,\theta(-t_{2}){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p_{0}t_{2}}={\rm i}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}t_{2}\,\frac{{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}(p_{0}-\omega)t_{2}}}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}={\rm i}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\frac{2\pi\delta(p_{0}-\omega)}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}},

(270)

obtaining

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&-\frac{1}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{2\pi\delta(p_{0}-\omega)}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p)\\
&\times\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)(y-x)^{\rho}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}.\end{split}

(271)

Then, noticing that

(y-x)^{\rho}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}={\rm i}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}

(272)

and integrating by parts, we obtain

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\frac{{\rm i}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}\,\frac{1}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}\left[\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p)\delta(p_{0}-\omega)\right]\\
&\times\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}.\end{split}

(273)

Figure 3:
Spacetime diagram of a relativistic heavy-ion collision. The hypersurface where LTE is achieved is
\Sigma_{\rm eq}=\Sigma(\tau)
(black line) and
\Sigma_{\rm FO}
(blue line) is the hypersurface where the quark-gluon plasma decouples and where spin polarization is evaluated. On the left we approximate the later time hypersurface
\Sigma=\Sigma_{\rm FO}
with an hyperplane orthogonal to
u
in
x
(red line). For the dissipative part where one needs to carry out the integral over the plasma volume
\Omega
, the volume is approximated as the flat volume within the two hyperplanes. On the right we approximate
\Sigma_{\rm FO}
as the spacelike hyperplane (red dashed line) with normal vector
\hat{t}
, the time direction in the center of mass frame, and at an average time
y^{0}_{\Sigma}
.

If
\Sigma
is the freezeout hypersurface in heavy ion collisions, see figure
3
, the integral in
\Sigma
can be obtained with the Gauss theorem, rewriting the integral in a flat plane at the initial phase of the collision and the difference is given by a volume integral
[
22
,
80
]
. In this way, one finds
[
80
]
:

\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)}=(2\pi)^{3}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p^{0}(y^{0}_{\Sigma}-x_{0})}\delta^{3}(\bm{p})\hat{t}_{\lambda},

(274)

where
\hat{t}
is the time direction in the center of mass frame and
y^{0}_{\Sigma}
denotes a constant time, dependent on
p
, which can be considered as an average time for the spacelike part of the hypersurface
\Sigma
, see figure
3
.
After integration first in
\bm{p}
and then in
p_{0}
we have

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\frac{{\rm i}\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int\frac{{\rm d}p_{0}}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p^{0}(y^{0}_{\Sigma}-x_{0})}\\
&\times\left[\delta(p_{0}-\omega)\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}+\eta^{\rho}_{0}\delta^{\prime}(p_{0}-\omega)\right]\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p_{0},\bm{p}=0)\\
=&\frac{{\rm i}\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\int\frac{{\rm d}p_{0}}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p^{0}(y^{0}_{\Sigma}-x_{0})}\delta(p_{0}-\omega)\\
&\times\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}-\eta^{\rho}_{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{0}}+{\rm i}(y^{0}_{\Sigma}-x_{0})\right]\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p_{0},\bm{p}=0)\\
=&\frac{{\rm i}\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm d}\omega}{2\pi}\frac{1}{\omega+{\rm i}0^{+}}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}-\eta^{\rho}_{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{0}}+{\rm i}(y^{0}_{\Sigma}-x_{0})\right]\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(\omega,\bm{p}=0).\end{split}

(275)

Given that
1/(\omega+{\rm i}0^{+})=
PV
(1/\omega)-{\rm i}\pi\delta(\omega)
and that the spectral function is real, we obtain

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\frac{\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{2|\beta(x)|}\lim_{\omega\to 0}\lim_{\bm{p}\to 0}\left[\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}-\eta^{\rho}_{0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{0}}\right]\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(\omega,\bm{p})\end{split}

(276)

or in covariant form and reminding that the spectral function is twice the imaginary part of the retarded function, we finally have:

\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=\frac{\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\lim_{p\cdot u\to 0}\lim_{p_{T}\to 0}\Delta^{\rho}_{\sigma}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\sigma}}{\rm Im}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\mathcal{U}}}(p).

(277)

If instead we would have approximated the integral over the hypersurface
\Sigma
as an integral over the hyperplane with normal
u(x)
, see figure
3
, then we could have used the translational invariance of the hyperplane to write:

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}\simeq&\frac{{\rm i}u_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}{\rm d}t_{2}\,\theta(-t_{2})\int\frac{{\rm d}^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}\rho^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}}(p)\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}^{3}y\,(y-x)^{\rho}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot(y-x)},\end{split}

(278)

and following the same steps as before we would have obtained:

\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},1}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=\frac{u_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\lim_{p\cdot u\to 0}\lim_{p_{T}\to 0}\frac{\partial}{\partial p_{\rho}}{\rm Im}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda(\alpha_{2})}_{\mathcal{U}}}(p).

(279)

The linear response to thermal vorticity is related to the conserved operator
\widehat{J}
, meaning that the result is independent on the specific form of the hypersurface
\Sigma
. Therefore, we can use the result above to compute its associated correlator.
The other type of correlators that we obtained for local thermal equilibrium is obtained from operators of the form

\widehat{B}_{\mathcal{U},0}=b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}(x)\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0}^{(\alpha)}=b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}(x)\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\lambda(\alpha)}(y),

(280)

that lead to

\Delta_{\mathcal{U},0,\,{\rm LTE}}O(x)=\mathcal{U}_{(\alpha)}(x)\,b_{\mathcal{U}}\,\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}

(281)

where

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=&\int_{0}^{|\beta|}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta(x)|}\langle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0[\tau/|\beta|]}^{(\alpha)}\widehat{O}(x)\rangle_{T(x),\,{\rm c}}\\
=&\int_{0}^{|\beta|}\frac{{\rm d}\tau}{|\beta(x)|}\int_{\Sigma}{\rm d}\Sigma_{\lambda}(y)\,\langle\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U}[\tau/|\beta|]}^{\lambda(\alpha)}(y)\widehat{O}(x)\rangle_{T(x),\,{\rm c}}\,.\end{split}

(282)

This can be worked out in a similar fashion to what described above, obtaining

\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}=\frac{\hat{t}_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\lim_{p\cdot u\to 0}\lim_{p_{T}\to 0}{\rm Re}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda(\alpha)}_{\mathcal{U}}}(p)

(283)

when approximating
\Sigma
with an average hyperplane orthogonal to
\hat{t}
, and

\begin{split}\left(\widehat{O},\,\widehat{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathcal{U},0}^{(\alpha)}\right)_{\rm LTE}\simeq&\frac{u_{\lambda}}{|\beta(x)|}\lim_{p\cdot u\to 0}\lim_{p_{T}\to 0}{\rm Re}\,G^{R}_{\widehat{O}\widehat{\mathcal{B}}^{\lambda(\alpha)}_{\mathcal{U}}}(p)\end{split}

(284)

when approximating the hypersurface with the hyperplane orthogonal to
u
evaluated in
x
.

Appendix B
Translations and Lorentz transformations of the Wigner operator

This appendix demonstrates that the axial and vector parts of the Wigner operator transform like a vector under Lorentz transformations. Recall that the Dirac field under translation transform as

\widehat{T}(a)\widehat{\psi}(x)\widehat{T}(a)^{\dagger}=\widehat{\psi}(x+a),\quad\widehat{T}(a)\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(x)\widehat{T}(a)^{\dagger}=\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(x+a),

(285)

and reminding the Wigner operator definition

\widehat{W}(x,k)=-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{4}y\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}k\cdot y}\widehat{\psi}(x-y/2)\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(x+y/2),

(286)

it follows that the translated Wigner operator is

\widehat{T}(a)\widehat{W}(x,k)\widehat{T}(a)^{\dagger}=\widehat{W}(x+a,k).

(287)

Similarly, under a Lorentz transformation, the Dirac field becomes

\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\psi}(x)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=S(\Lambda)\widehat{\psi}(\Lambda^{-1}x),\quad\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(x)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(\Lambda^{-1}x)S(\Lambda^{-1}),

(288)

then we can derive how the Wigner function transforms:

\begin{split}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{W}(x,k)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=&-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{4}y\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}k\cdot y}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\psi}(x-y/2)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(x+y/2)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}\\
=&-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{4}y\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}k\cdot y}S(\Lambda)\widehat{\psi}(\Lambda^{-1}x-\Lambda^{-1}y/2)\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(\Lambda^{-1}x+\Lambda^{-1}y/2)S(\Lambda^{-1})\\
=&-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{4}y^{\prime}\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}k\cdot\Lambda y^{\prime}}S(\Lambda)\widehat{\psi}(\Lambda^{-1}x-y^{\prime}/2)\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(\Lambda^{-1}x+y^{\prime}/2)S(\Lambda^{-1})\\
=&-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int{\rm d}^{4}y\,{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\Lambda^{-1}k\cdot y}S(\Lambda)\widehat{\psi}(\Lambda^{-1}x-y/2)\widehat{\bar{\psi}}(\Lambda^{-1}x+y/2)S(\Lambda^{-1})\\
=&S(\Lambda)\widehat{W}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\,\Lambda^{-1}k)S(\Lambda^{-1}).\end{split}

(289)

The axial part of the Wigner operator is defined as

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}(x,k)={\rm tr}_{4}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}\widehat{W}(x,k)\right]

(290)

from which, using the previous relations, it follows that

\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu}(x,k)={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\widehat{P}\cdot x}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu}(0,k)\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\widehat{P}\cdot x}

(291)

and

\begin{split}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\mu}(x,k)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=&{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{W}(x,k)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}\right]={\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}S(\Lambda)\widehat{W}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\Lambda^{-1}k)S(\Lambda^{-1})\right]\\
=&{\rm tr}\left[S(\Lambda^{-1})\gamma^{\mu}\gamma^{5}S(\Lambda)\widehat{W}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\Lambda^{-1}k)\right]=\Lambda^{\mu}_{\,\nu}{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{\nu}\gamma^{5}\widehat{W}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\Lambda^{-1}k)\right]\\
=&\Lambda^{\mu}_{\,\nu}\widehat{\mathcal{A}}^{\nu}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\Lambda^{-1}k).\end{split}

(292)

In the same fashion, one can show that

\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu}(x,k)={\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\widehat{P}\cdot x}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu}(0,k)\,{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\widehat{P}\cdot x},\quad\widehat{\Lambda}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\mu}(x,k)\widehat{\Lambda}^{\dagger}=\Lambda^{\mu}_{\,\nu}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{\nu}(\Lambda^{-1}x,\Lambda^{-1}k).

(293)

Appendix C
Chiral spin Hall effect conductivities

This appendix calculates the conductivities related to the spin Hall like effects; that is

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{A}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\left[a^{c}_{r\epsilon}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta+\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{A}\right],

(294)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{V}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\left[\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta+v^{c}_{r_{A}\epsilon}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{A}\right],

(295)

for a fluid composed of a non-interacting massless Dirac particle and with chiral imbalance
\zeta_{A}
. The Kubo formulas for the conductivities are given in eqs. (
149d
) and (
149e
) that read

\displaystyle a_{r\epsilon}^{c}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k),\,\widehat{j}^{\sigma}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE},

(296)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k),\,\widehat{j}^{\sigma}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE},

(297)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{A}}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k),\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\sigma}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE},

(298)

\displaystyle v^{c}_{r_{A}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k),\,\widehat{j}_{A}^{\sigma}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE},

(299)

where, see eq. (
43
),

\left(\widehat{X}(x,k),\,\widehat{Y}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE}=\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}z\,\langle\widehat{Y}(y+{\rm i}z\beta)\widehat{X}(x)\rangle_{\beta(x),\,{\rm c}}\,.

(300)

The thermal expectation values are obtained with the statistical operator of equilibrium including the axial chemical potential, that is

\widehat{\rho}_{{\rm eq}(x)}=\frac{1}{\mathcal{Z}_{\rm eq}}\exp\left\{-\beta(x)\cdot\widehat{P}+\zeta(x)\widehat{Q}+\zeta_{A}(x)\widehat{Q}_{A}\right\}.

(301)

For a massless field, instead of using the vector and axial currents and the vector and axial parts of the Wigner function, it is more convenient to use the right- and left-handed chiralities:

\displaystyle\mu=

\displaystyle\mu_{R}+\mu_{L},

\displaystyle\widehat{j}^{\mu}(y)=

\displaystyle\widehat{j}_{R}^{\mu}(y)+\widehat{j}_{L}^{\mu}(y),

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)+\mathcal{L}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k),

(302)

\displaystyle\mu_{A}=

\displaystyle\mu_{R}-\mu_{L},

\displaystyle\widehat{j}_{A}^{\mu}(y)=

\displaystyle\widehat{j}_{R}^{\mu}(y)-\widehat{j}_{L}^{\mu}(y),

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)-\mathcal{L}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k).

(303)

Using the right and left chiralities, the effects in eqs. (
294
) and (
295
) are translated to

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{R}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{V}_{R\,+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\left[v_{R,R}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{R}+v_{R,L}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{L}\right],

(305)

\displaystyle\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{L}_{+}^{\mu}(x,k)=\Delta_{\rm SHE}\mathcal{V}_{L\,+}^{\mu}(x,k)=

\displaystyle\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}\left[v_{L,R}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{r}+v_{L,L}(k)\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{L}\right],

(306)

or altogether, denoting with
\chi,\,\chi^{\prime}=+1,\,-1=R,\,L
corresponding respectively to right and left chirality,

\Delta_{\chi^{\prime}}\mathcal{V}_{\chi\,+}^{\mu}(x,k)=\epsilon^{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}\frac{k^{\perp}_{\nu}u_{\sigma}}{(k\cdot u)}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}\partial_{\rho}\zeta_{\chi^{\prime}}

(307)

with the conductivity obtained with

v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left(\widehat{\mathcal{V}}_{\chi\,+}^{\mu}(x,k),\,\widehat{j}_{\chi^{\prime}}^{\sigma}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE}.

(308)

Replacing the definitions of the current and of the right/left part of Wigner function, the conductivity is

\begin{split}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=&\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1+\chi\gamma^{5}}{2}\right]_{BA}\\
&\times\left(\widehat{W}^{+}_{AB}(x,k),\,\bar{\widehat{\Psi}}(y)\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5}}{2}\gamma^{\sigma}\widehat{\Psi}(y)\right)_{\rm LTE}.\end{split}

(309)

Then, using the normal modes expansion of the free Dirac field:

\widehat{\Psi}_{+}(x)=\sum_{h}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3/2}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}u_{h}(p){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}p\cdot x}\widehat{a}_{h}(p)

(310)

with
h
their chirality, we obtain

\widehat{W}^{+}_{AB}(x,k)=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\sum_{h,h^{\prime}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}}\delta\left(k-\frac{p+p^{\prime}}{2}\right){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}x(p^{\prime}-p)}u_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})_{A}\bar{u}_{h}(p)_{B}\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{h}(p)\widehat{a}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime}),

(311)

and

\widehat{j}^{\sigma}_{\chi^{\prime}\,+}(y+{\rm i}z\beta)=\sum_{\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}q}{2\varepsilon_{q}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{q^{\prime}}}\frac{{\rm e}^{{\rm i}y(q^{\prime}-q)-z\beta\cdot(q^{\prime}-q)}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\bar{u}_{\tau^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5}}{2}u_{\tau}(q)\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{\tau^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})\widehat{a}_{\tau}(q),

(312)

where we neglected the antiparticle part of the current because its contribution vanish when correlated with the particle part of the Wigner function. Replacing these expressions in the conductivity, we obtain

\begin{split}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=&\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1+\chi\gamma^{5}}{2}\right]_{BA}\left[\gamma^{\sigma}\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5}}{2}\right]_{B^{\prime}A^{\prime}}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\\
&\times\sum_{h,h^{\prime},\tau,\tau^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}z\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}q}{2\varepsilon_{q}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}q^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{q^{\prime}}}\delta\left(k-\frac{p+p^{\prime}}{2}\right){\rm e}^{-{\rm i}x(p^{\prime}-p)}{\rm e}^{({\rm i}y-z\beta)\cdot(q^{\prime}-q)}\\
&\times u_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})_{A}\bar{u}_{\tau^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})_{B^{\prime}}u_{\tau}(q)_{A^{\prime}}\bar{u}_{h}(p)_{B}\langle\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{h}(p)\widehat{a}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{\tau^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})\widehat{a}_{\tau}(q)\rangle_{\beta,\,c}.\end{split}

(313)

Using
\langle\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{1}\,\widehat{a}_{2}\,\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{3}\,\widehat{a}_{4}\rangle_{\beta,\,c}=\langle\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{1}\,\widehat{a}_{4}\rangle_{\beta}\langle\widehat{a}_{2}\,\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{3}\rangle_{\beta}
, and the expectation values with the statistical operator (
301
), that is

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{h}(p)\widehat{a}_{\tau}(q)\rangle_{\beta}=

\displaystyle\delta_{h\tau}2\varepsilon_{q}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{q})n^{h}_{F}(x,p),

(314)

\displaystyle\langle\widehat{a}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\widehat{a}^{\dagger}_{\tau^{\prime}}(q^{\prime})\rangle_{\beta}=

\displaystyle\delta_{h^{\prime}\tau^{\prime}}2\varepsilon_{q^{\prime}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}^{\prime}-\bm{q}^{\prime})(1-n^{h^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p^{\prime})),

(315)

where

n^{\chi}_{F}(x,k)=\frac{1}{{\rm e}^{\beta(x)\cdot k-\zeta(x)-\chi\zeta_{A}(x)}+1},

(316)

we can immediately integrate over
q
and
q^{\prime}
and sum over
\tau
and
\tau^{\prime}
. We now consider the sums over the chiralities and we construct the trace over the spinoral indices:

\begin{split}\sum_{h,h^{\prime}}&{\rm tr}\left[\bar{u}_{h}(p)\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1+\chi\gamma^{5}}{2}u_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\bar{u}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5}}{2}u_{h}(p)\right]n^{h}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{h^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\\
=&\sum_{h,h^{\prime}}{\rm tr}\left[\bar{u}_{h}(p)\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1+\chi h^{\prime}}{2}u_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\bar{u}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}h}{2}u_{h}(p)\right]n^{h}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{h^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\\
=&\sum_{h,h^{\prime}}\delta_{h^{\prime},\chi}\delta_{h,\chi^{\prime}}{\rm tr}\left[\bar{u}_{h}(p)\gamma^{\mu}u_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\bar{u}_{h^{\prime}}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}u_{h}(p)\right]n^{h}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{h^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\\
=&{\rm tr}\left[\bar{u}_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)\gamma^{\mu}u_{\chi}(p^{\prime})\bar{u}_{\chi}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}u_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)\right]n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime})),\end{split}

(317)

where we used
\gamma^{5}u_{h}(p)=h\,u_{h}(p)
. Now, using the cyclicity of the trace and
u_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)\bar{u}_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)=(1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5})\not{p}/2
, we obtain

\begin{split}T^{\mu\sigma}(p,p^{\prime};\chi,\chi^{\prime})=&{\rm tr}\left[\bar{u}_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)\gamma^{\mu}u_{\chi}(p^{\prime})\bar{u}_{\chi}(p^{\prime})\gamma^{\sigma}u_{\chi^{\prime}}(p)\right]\\
=&{\rm tr}\left[\gamma^{\mu}\frac{1+\chi\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}^{\prime}\gamma^{\sigma}\frac{1+\chi^{\prime}\gamma^{5}}{2}\not{p}\right]\\
=&(1+\chi\chi^{\prime})(p^{\mu}p^{\prime\sigma}+p^{\prime\mu}p^{\sigma}-(p\cdot p^{\prime})\eta^{\mu\sigma})+{\rm i}\epsilon^{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}p_{\alpha}p^{\prime}_{\beta}.\end{split}

(318)

After these steps we obtained

\begin{split}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=&\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}u^{\lambda}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k_{\perp}^{\tau}\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{6}}\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}z\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}}\delta\left(k-\frac{p+p^{\prime}}{2}\right)\\
&\times T^{\mu\sigma}(p,p^{\prime};\chi,\chi^{\prime})n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime})){\rm e}^{-z\beta\cdot(p^{\prime}-p)}\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}(p^{\prime}-p)(x-y)}.\end{split}

(319)

The integral over the hypersurface gives

\int{\rm d}\Sigma_{\sigma}(y-x)^{\langle\rho\rangle}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}(p^{\prime}-p)(x-y)}=-{\rm i}u_{\sigma}\Delta^{\rho}_{\,\kappa}(2\pi)^{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\prime}_{\kappa}}\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{p}^{\prime}).

(320)

The derivative of the Dirac delta can be integrated by parts. After that, it is easy to use the deltas to integrate in
p
and
p^{\prime}
, resulting in
p=p^{\prime}=k
. Noticing that

\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u_{\lambda}u_{\sigma}k_{\perp}^{\tau}T^{\mu\sigma}(p=k,p^{\prime}=k;\chi,\chi^{\prime})=2(1+\chi\chi^{\prime})(k\cdot u)\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u^{\lambda}k_{\perp}^{\tau}k^{\mu}=0,

(321)

the only non-vanishing contribution is given by

\begin{split}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=&{\rm i}\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u^{\lambda}k_{\perp}^{\tau}u_{\sigma}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm d}z}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}}{\rm e}^{z\beta\cdot(p-p^{\prime})}\delta\left(k-\frac{p+p^{\prime}}{2}\right)\\
&\times\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{p}^{\prime})n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\prime}_{\langle\rho\rangle}}T^{\mu\sigma}(p,p^{\prime};\chi,\chi^{\prime}).\end{split}

(322)

The derivatives in
p^{\prime}
must be carried out reminding that
p^{\prime}
is on-shell:

\frac{\partial p^{\prime\sigma}}{\partial p^{\prime}_{\langle\rho\rangle}}=\Delta^{\sigma}_{\,\rho}-u^{\sigma}\frac{p^{\prime\langle\rho\rangle}}{\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}},

(323)

therefore, after integrating in
p^{\prime}
, one obtains

\begin{split}\left.\frac{\partial}{\partial p^{\prime}_{\langle\rho\rangle}}T^{\mu\sigma}(p,p^{\prime};\chi,\chi^{\prime})\right|_{p^{\prime}=p}=&(1+\chi^{\prime}\chi^{\prime})\left(p^{\mu}\frac{\partial p^{\sigma}}{\partial p_{\langle\rho\rangle}}+\frac{\partial p^{\mu}}{\partial p_{\langle\rho\rangle}}p^{\sigma}-p_{\alpha}\frac{\partial p^{\alpha}}{\partial p_{\langle\rho\rangle}}\eta^{\mu\sigma}\right)\\
&+{\rm i}(\chi+\chi^{\prime})\epsilon^{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}p_{\alpha}\frac{\partial p_{\beta}}{\partial p_{\langle\rho\rangle}}.\end{split}

(324)

After integration in
p
, that sets
p=k
, the first term in the round bracket gives a vanishing contribution to the conductivity; indeed:

\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}k^{\tau}k^{\mu}=0.

(325)

The same happens for the second:

\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u^{\lambda}(\Delta^{\mu}_{\,\rho}-u^{\mu}\frac{k^{\langle\rho\rangle}}{\varepsilon_{k}})=0,

(326)

and the third:

\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u^{\lambda}u_{\sigma}\eta^{\mu\sigma}=0.

(327)

Therefore, only the last term gives non-vanishing contributions and the conductivity reads

\begin{split}v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=&{\rm i}\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}u^{\lambda}k_{\perp}^{\tau}u_{\sigma}\int_{0}^{1}\frac{{\rm d}z}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p}{2\varepsilon_{p}}\int\frac{{\rm d}^{3}p^{\prime}}{2\varepsilon_{p^{\prime}}}{\rm e}^{z\beta\cdot(p-p^{\prime})}\delta\left(k-\frac{p+p^{\prime}}{2}\right)\\
&\times\delta^{3}(\bm{p}-\bm{p}^{\prime})n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime})){\rm i}(\chi+\chi^{\prime})\epsilon^{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}p_{\alpha}\frac{\partial p^{\prime}_{\beta}}{\partial p^{\prime}_{\langle\rho\rangle}}\\
=&-\frac{\chi+\chi^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}\epsilon^{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}u^{\lambda}k_{\perp}^{\tau}k_{\alpha}\left(\Delta^{\rho}_{\,\beta}-u_{\beta}\frac{k^{\langle\rho\rangle}}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right)n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\\
&\times\int_{0}^{1}{\rm d}z\,\frac{\delta(k\cdot u-\varepsilon_{k})}{2(k\cdot u)}\frac{1}{2(k\cdot u)}\\
=&-\frac{\chi+\chi^{\prime}}{2}\frac{(k\cdot u)}{2k_{\perp}^{2}}\epsilon_{\lambda\mu\tau\rho}\epsilon^{\mu\sigma\alpha\beta}u^{\lambda}k_{\perp}^{\tau}k_{\alpha}\left(\Delta^{\rho}_{\,\beta}-u_{\beta}\frac{k^{\langle\rho\rangle}}{\varepsilon_{k}}\right)n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime}))\\
&\times\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{\delta(k\cdot u-\varepsilon_{k})}{2(k\cdot u)}.\end{split}

(328)

After carrying out the contractions, one obtains

v_{\chi,\chi^{\prime}}=-\frac{\chi+\chi^{\prime}}{2}\frac{\delta(k^{2})\theta(k\cdot u)}{(2\pi)^{3}}n^{\chi^{\prime}}_{F}(x,p)(1-n^{\chi}_{F}(x,p^{\prime})).

(329)

We note that the conductivities are vanishing when
\chi^{\prime}=-\chi
. To obtain the original conductivities in eqs. (
294
) and (
295
) we simply have (in compact notation):

\displaystyle a_{r\epsilon}^{c}=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}-\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}+\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)+\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)

(330)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=v_{R,R}-v_{L,L}

(331)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta(k^{2})\theta(k\cdot u)}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left[n_{F}^{R}(x,k)(1-n_{F}^{R}(x,k))+n_{F}^{L}(x,k)(1-n_{F}^{L}(x,k))\right]

(332)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}-\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=v^{c}_{r_{A}\epsilon},

(333)

and

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}_{r_{A}\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}-\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}-\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)-\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)+\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)

(334)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}\right)+\left(\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=v_{R,R}+v_{L,L}

(335)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{\delta(k^{2})\theta(k\cdot u)}{(2\pi)^{3}}\left[n_{F}^{R}(x,k)(1-n_{F}^{R}(x,k))-n_{F}^{L}(x,k)(1-n_{F}^{L}(x,k))\right]

(336)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathcal{R}}+\widehat{\mathcal{L}},\,\widehat{j}_{R}+\widehat{j}_{L}\right)=\mathfrak{v}_{r\epsilon}.

(337)

These are the values reported in section
5.2
.
